					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Stanley Baldwin</span></h1>

<div><span typeof="mw:File"><span><audio id="mwe_player_0" controls="" preload="none" class="mw-file-element" width="215" style="width:215px;" data-durationhint="195" data-mwtitle="Stanley_Baldwin&#39;s_last_speech_as_Prime_Minister.ogg" data-mwprovider="wikimediacommons"><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f6/Stanley_Baldwin%27s_last_speech_as_Prime_Minister.ogg" type="audio/ogg; codecs=&quot;vorbis&quot;" data-title="Original Ogg file (63 kbps)" data-shorttitle="Ogg source" data-width="0" data-height="0" /><source src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/f/f6/Stanley_Baldwin%27s_last_speech_as_Prime_Minister.ogg/Stanley_Baldwin%27s_last_speech_as_Prime_Minister.ogg.mp3" type="audio/mpeg" data-title="MP3" data-shorttitle="MP3" data-transcodekey="mp3" data-width="0" data-height="0" /></audio></span></span></div>

</div></td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-below" style="border-top: 1px solid right; font-size: 95%"><div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Stanley Baldwin, 1st Earl Baldwin of Bewdley</b>, <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:100%; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Knight_of_the_Order_of_the_Garter" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight of the Order of the Garter">KG</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">PC</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Queen%27s_Privy_Council_for_Canada" class="mw-redirect" title="Queen&#39;s Privy Council for Canada">PC (Can)</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Justice_of_the_Peace#United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Justice of the Peace">JP</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Fellow_of_the_Royal_Society" title="Fellow of the Royal Society">FRS</a></span> (3 August 1867&#160;&#8211;&#32;14 December 1947)<sup id="cite_ref-Stuart_Ball_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stuart_Ball-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> was a British statesman and <a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_(UK)" title="Conservative Party (UK)">Conservative</a> politician who dominated the government of the <a href="/wiki/Interwar_Britain" title="Interwar Britain">United Kingdom between the world wars</a>, serving as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">prime minister</a> on three occasions, from May 1923 to January 1924, from November 1924 to June 1929, and from June 1935 to May 1937.
</p><p>Born to a prosperous family in <a href="/wiki/Bewdley" title="Bewdley">Bewdley</a>, Worcestershire, Baldwin was educated at <a href="/wiki/Hawtreys" title="Hawtreys">Hawtreys</a>, <a href="/wiki/Harrow_School" title="Harrow School">Harrow School</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Trinity College, Cambridge</a>. He joined the family iron and steel making business and entered the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="House of Commons of the United Kingdom">House of Commons</a> in <a href="/wiki/1908_Bewdley_by-election" title="1908 Bewdley by-election">1908</a> as the member for <a href="/wiki/Bewdley_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Bewdley (UK Parliament constituency)">Bewdley</a>, succeeding his father <a href="/wiki/Alfred_Baldwin_(politician)" title="Alfred Baldwin (politician)">Alfred</a>. He served as <a href="/wiki/Financial_Secretary_to_the_Treasury" title="Financial Secretary to the Treasury">Financial Secretary to the Treasury</a> (1917–1921) and <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Board_of_Trade" title="President of the Board of Trade">President of the Board of Trade</a> (1921–1922) in the <a href="/wiki/Lloyd_George_ministry" title="Lloyd George ministry">coalition ministry</a> of <a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George" title="David Lloyd George">David Lloyd George</a> and then rose rapidly: in 1922, Baldwin was one of the prime movers in the <a href="/wiki/Carlton_Club_meeting" title="Carlton Club meeting">withdrawal of Conservative support</a> from Lloyd George; he subsequently became <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bonar_Law" title="Bonar Law">Bonar Law</a>'s Conservative ministry. Upon Law's resignation for health reasons in May 1923, Baldwin became prime minister and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_Conservative_Party_(UK)" title="Leader of the Conservative Party (UK)">leader of the Conservative Party</a>. He called an <a href="/wiki/1923_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1923 United Kingdom general election">election in December 1923</a> on the issue of <a href="/wiki/Tariff_Reform_League" title="Tariff Reform League">tariffs</a> and lost the Conservatives' parliamentary majority, after which <a href="/wiki/Ramsay_MacDonald" title="Ramsay MacDonald">Ramsay MacDonald</a> formed a minority <a href="/wiki/Labour_Party_(UK)" title="Labour Party (UK)">Labour</a> government.
</p><p>After winning the <a href="/wiki/1924_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1924 United Kingdom general election">1924 general election</a>, Baldwin formed his second government, which saw important tenures of office by Sir <a href="/wiki/Austen_Chamberlain" title="Austen Chamberlain">Austen Chamberlain</a> (Foreign Secretary), <a href="/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill">Winston Churchill</a> (at the Exchequer) and <a href="/wiki/Neville_Chamberlain" title="Neville Chamberlain">Neville Chamberlain</a> (Health). The latter two ministers strengthened Conservative appeal by reforms in areas formerly associated with the Liberal Party. They included industrial conciliation, unemployment insurance, a more extensive old-age pension system, <a href="/wiki/Slum_clearance_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Slum clearance in the United Kingdom">slum clearance</a>, more private housing and expansion of maternal care and childcare. However, continuing sluggish economic growth and declines in mining and heavy industry weakened Baldwin's base of support. His government also saw the <a href="/wiki/1926_United_Kingdom_general_strike" title="1926 United Kingdom general strike">General Strike</a> in 1926 and introduced the <a href="/wiki/Trade_Disputes_and_Trade_Unions_Act_1927" title="Trade Disputes and Trade Unions Act 1927">Trade Disputes and Trade Unions Act 1927</a> to curb the powers of trade unions.<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Baldwin narrowly lost the <a href="/wiki/1929_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1929 United Kingdom general election">1929 general election</a> and his continued leadership of the party was subject to extensive criticism by press barons <a href="/wiki/Harold_Harmsworth,_1st_Viscount_Rothermere" title="Harold Harmsworth, 1st Viscount Rothermere">Lord Rothermere</a> and <a href="/wiki/Max_Aitken,_1st_Baron_Beaverbrook" title="Max Aitken, 1st Baron Beaverbrook">Lord Beaverbrook</a>. In 1931, with the onset of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Depression" title="Great Depression">Great Depression</a>, Labour Prime Minister Ramsay MacDonald formed a <a href="/wiki/National_Government_(United_Kingdom)" title="National Government (United Kingdom)">National Government</a>, most of whose ministers were Conservatives, and which won an enormous majority at the <a href="/wiki/1931_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1931 United Kingdom general election">1931 general election</a>. As <a href="/wiki/Lord_President_of_the_Council" title="Lord President of the Council">Lord President of the Council</a> and one of four Conservatives among the small ten-member Cabinet, Baldwin took over many of the Prime Minister's duties when MacDonald's health deteriorated. This government saw <a href="/wiki/Government_of_India_Act_1935" title="Government of India Act 1935">an Act</a> delivering increased self-government for India, a measure opposed by Churchill and by many rank-and-file Conservatives. The <a href="/wiki/Statute_of_Westminster_1931" title="Statute of Westminster 1931">Statute of Westminster 1931</a> gave <a href="/wiki/Dominion" title="Dominion">Dominion</a> status to Canada,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Australia, New Zealand, and South Africa, while taking the first step towards the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>. As party leader, Baldwin made many striking innovations, such as clever use of radio and film, that made him highly visible to the public and strengthened Conservative appeal.
</p><p>In 1935, Baldwin replaced MacDonald as prime minister and won the <a href="/wiki/1935_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1935 United Kingdom general election">1935 general election</a> with another large majority. During this time, he oversaw the beginning of British rearmament and the <a href="/wiki/Abdication_of_King_Edward_VIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Abdication of King Edward VIII">abdication of King Edward VIII</a>. Baldwin's third government saw a number of crises in foreign affairs, including the public uproar over the <a href="/wiki/Hoare%E2%80%93Laval_Pact" title="Hoare–Laval Pact">Hoare–Laval Pact</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Remilitarisation_of_the_Rhineland" class="mw-redirect" title="Remilitarisation of the Rhineland">remilitarisation of the Rhineland</a>, and the outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Civil_War" title="Spanish Civil War">Spanish Civil War</a>. Baldwin retired in 1937 and was succeeded by Neville Chamberlain. At that time, Baldwin was regarded as a popular and successful prime minister,<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> but for the final decade of his life and for many years afterwards he was vilified for having presided over high unemployment in the 1930s and as one of several British public figures who had tried to appease <a href="/wiki/Adolf_Hitler" title="Adolf Hitler">Adolf Hitler</a> and who had supposedly not rearmed sufficiently to prepare for the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>. Today, modern scholars <a href="/wiki/Historical_rankings_of_prime_ministers_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Historical rankings of prime ministers of the United Kingdom">generally rank him in the upper half</a> of British prime ministers.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span id="Early_life:_family.2C_education_and_marriage"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life:_family,_education_and_marriage">Early life: family, education and marriage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life: family, education and marriage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Baldwin was born at Lower Park House (Lower Park, <a href="/wiki/Bewdley" title="Bewdley">Bewdley</a>) in <a href="/wiki/Worcestershire" title="Worcestershire">Worcestershire</a>, England, to <a href="/wiki/Alfred_Baldwin_(politician)" title="Alfred Baldwin (politician)">Alfred</a> and <a href="/wiki/MacDonald_sisters" title="MacDonald sisters">Louisa (MacDonald) Baldwin</a>, and through his mother was a first cousin of the writer and poet <a href="/wiki/Rudyard_Kipling" title="Rudyard Kipling">Rudyard Kipling</a>, with whom he was close for their entire lives. A summer spent with Kipling and his sister with the freedom of farm and forest at <a href="/wiki/Loughton" title="Loughton">Loughton</a> in 1877 was seminal in the development of both boys.<sup id="cite_ref-Lingley_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lingley-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The family was prosperous, and owned the eponymous iron and steel making business that in later years became part of <a href="/wiki/Richard_Thomas_and_Baldwins" title="Richard Thomas and Baldwins">Richard Thomas and Baldwins</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Baldwin's schools were <a href="/wiki/Hawtreys" title="Hawtreys">St Michael's School</a>, at the time located in <a href="/wiki/Slough" title="Slough">Slough</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buckinghamshire" title="Buckinghamshire">Buckinghamshire</a> (now <a href="/wiki/Berkshire" title="Berkshire">Berkshire</a>), followed by <a href="/wiki/Harrow_School" title="Harrow School">Harrow School</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> He later wrote that "<a href="/wiki/Humpty_Dumpty" title="Humpty Dumpty">all the king's horses and all the king's men</a> would have failed to have drawn me into the company of school masters, and in relation to them I once had every qualification as a passive resister."<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Baldwin then went on to the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Cambridge" title="University of Cambridge">University of Cambridge</a>, where he studied history at <a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Trinity College</a>. His time at university was blighted by the presence, as <a href="/wiki/Trinity_College,_Cambridge#Masters" title="Trinity College, Cambridge">Master of Trinity</a>, of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Montagu_Butler" title="Henry Montagu Butler">Henry Montagu Butler</a>, his former headmaster who had punished him at Harrow for writing a piece of schoolboy smut. He was asked to resign from the Magpie &amp; Stump (the Trinity College debating society) for never speaking, and, after receiving a <a href="/wiki/Third-class_degree" class="mw-redirect" title="Third-class degree">third-class degree</a> in history, he went into the family business of iron manufacturing. His father sent him to <a href="/wiki/Mason_Science_College" title="Mason Science College">Mason College</a> for one session of technical training in <a href="/wiki/Metallurgy" title="Metallurgy">metallurgy</a> as preparation.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> As a young man he served briefly as a <a href="/wiki/Second_lieutenant" title="Second lieutenant">second lieutenant</a> in the Artillery Volunteers at <a href="/wiki/Malvern,_Worcestershire" title="Malvern, Worcestershire">Malvern</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> and in 1897 became a <a href="/wiki/Justice_of_the_peace" title="Justice of the peace">JP</a> for the county of <a href="/wiki/Worcestershire" title="Worcestershire">Worcestershire</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-WWW_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WWW-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Baldwin married <a href="/wiki/Lucy_Baldwin" title="Lucy Baldwin">Lucy Ridsdale</a> on 12 September 1892. Following the birth of a still-born son in January 1894, the couple had six surviving children:<sup id="cite_ref-Stuart_Ball_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stuart_Ball-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Lady Diana Lucy Baldwin (8 April 1895 – 21 May 1982)</li>
<li>Lady Leonora Stanley Baldwin (10 July 1896 – 23 June 1989)</li>
<li>Lady Pamela Margaret Baldwin (16 September 1897 – 14 August 1976)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Major" title="Major">Major</a> <a href="/wiki/Oliver_Baldwin,_2nd_Earl_Baldwin_of_Bewdley" title="Oliver Baldwin, 2nd Earl Baldwin of Bewdley">Oliver Ridsdale Baldwin, 2nd Earl Baldwin of Bewdley</a> (1 March 1899 – 10 August 1958)</li>
<li>Lady Esther Louisa (Betty) Baldwin (16 March 1902 – 22 June 1981)<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Arthur_Baldwin,_3rd_Earl_Baldwin_of_Bewdley" title="Arthur Baldwin, 3rd Earl Baldwin of Bewdley">Arthur Windham Baldwin, 3rd Earl Baldwin of Bewdley</a> (22 March 1904 – 5 July 1976)</li></ul>
<p>Baldwin's youngest daughter, Lady Betty, was severely injured by shrapnel in March 1941 as a result of a <a href="/wiki/The_Blitz" title="The Blitz">bombing raid</a> which destroyed the <a href="/wiki/Caf%C3%A9_de_Paris_(London)" class="mw-redirect" title="Café de Paris (London)">Café de Paris</a> nightclub she was attending and decapitated the famous bandleader <a href="/wiki/Ken_%22Snakehips%22_Johnson" title="Ken &quot;Snakehips&quot; Johnson">Ken "Snakehips" Johnson</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Belton,_Neil_1998,_p.52_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Belton,_Neil_1998,_p.52-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> She required facial reconstruction surgery from the pioneering surgeon <a href="/wiki/Archibald_MacIndoe" class="mw-redirect" title="Archibald MacIndoe">Archibald MacIndoe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Belton,_Neil_1998,_p.52_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Belton,_Neil_1998,_p.52-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Baldwin proved to be adept as a businessman, and acquired a reputation as a modernising industrialist. He inherited £200,000, equivalent to £22,215,635&#32;in 2021,<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> and a directorship of the <a href="/wiki/Great_Western_Railway" title="Great Western Railway">Great Western Railway</a> on the death of his father in 1908.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_political_career">Early political career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early political career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Member_of_Parliament">Member of Parliament</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Member of Parliament">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/1906_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1906 United Kingdom general election">1906 general election</a> he contested <a href="/wiki/Kidderminster_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Kidderminster (UK Parliament constituency)">Kidderminster</a> but lost amidst the <a href="/wiki/Conservative_Party_(UK)" title="Conservative Party (UK)">Conservative</a> landslide defeat after the party split on the issue of free trade. In <a href="/wiki/1908_Bewdley_by-election" title="1908 Bewdley by-election">a by-election in 1908</a> he was elected <a href="/wiki/Member_of_Parliament_(United_Kingdom)" title="Member of Parliament (United Kingdom)">Member of Parliament</a> (MP) for <a href="/wiki/Bewdley_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Bewdley (UK Parliament constituency)">Bewdley</a>, in which role he succeeded his father, who had died earlier that year. During the First World War he became <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_Private_Secretary" title="Parliamentary Private Secretary">Parliamentary Private Secretary</a> to the party leader <a href="/wiki/Bonar_Law" title="Bonar Law">Bonar Law</a>. In 1917 he was appointed to the junior ministerial post of <a href="/wiki/Financial_Secretary_to_the_Treasury" title="Financial Secretary to the Treasury">Financial Secretary to the Treasury</a>, where he sought to encourage voluntary donations by the rich to repay the United Kingdom's war debt, writing letters to <i><a href="/wiki/The_Times" title="The Times">The Times</a></i> under the pseudonym 'FST', many of which were published. He relinquished to the Treasury one fifth of his own fortune (its total estimated at own account as £580,000) held in the form of <a href="/wiki/War_Loan" class="mw-redirect" title="War Loan">War Loan</a> stock worth £120,000.<sup id="cite_ref-Stuart_Ball_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stuart_Ball-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Joins_Cabinet">Joins Cabinet</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Joins Cabinet">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Astley,_Worcs,_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg/220px-Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg/330px-Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg/440px-Astley%2C_Worcs%2C_Astley_Hall_2_back.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3592" data-file-height="2360" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Astley_Hall_(Stourport-on-Severn)" class="mw-redirect" title="Astley Hall (Stourport-on-Severn)">Astley Hall</a> near <a href="/wiki/Stourport_On_Severn" class="mw-redirect" title="Stourport On Severn">Stourport On Severn</a>, Baldwin's home between 1902 and 1947</figcaption></figure>
<p>Although he entered politics at a relatively late age, his rise to the top leadership was very rapid. In the Treasury he served jointly with <a href="/wiki/Hardman_Lever" title="Hardman Lever">Sir Hardman Lever</a>, who had been appointed in 1916, but after 1919 Baldwin carried out the duties largely alone. He was appointed to the <a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">Privy Council</a> in the <a href="/wiki/1920_Birthday_Honours" title="1920 Birthday Honours">1920 Birthday Honours</a>. In 1921 he was promoted to <a href="/wiki/Conservative_government_1922%E2%80%9324" class="mw-redirect" title="Conservative government 1922–24">the Cabinet</a> as <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Board_of_Trade" title="President of the Board of Trade">President of the Board of Trade</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Chancellor of the Exchequer">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In late 1922 dissatisfaction was steadily growing within the Conservative Party over its coalition with the <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Party_(UK)" title="Liberal Party (UK)">Liberal</a> <a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George" title="David Lloyd George">David Lloyd George</a>. At a <a href="/wiki/Carlton_Club_meeting,_19_October_1922" class="mw-redirect" title="Carlton Club meeting, 19 October 1922">meeting of Conservative MPs</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Carlton_Club" title="Carlton Club">Carlton Club</a> in October, Baldwin announced that he would no longer support the coalition, and famously condemned Lloyd George for being a "dynamic force" that was bringing destruction across politics. The meeting chose to leave the coalition, against the wishes of most of the party leadership. As a direct result Bonar Law was forced to search for new ministers for a Cabinet which he would lead, and so promoted Baldwin to the position of <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a>. In the November <a href="/wiki/1922_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1922 United Kingdom general election">1922 general election</a> the Conservatives were returned with a majority in their own right.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_minister:_first_term_.281923.E2.80.931924.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_minister:_first_term_(1923–1924)">Prime minister: first term (1923–1924)<span class="anchor" id="First_premiership"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Prime minister: first term (1923–1924)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Appointment">Appointment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Appointment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/First_Baldwin_ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="First Baldwin ministry">First Baldwin ministry</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg/220px-Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="300" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg/330px-Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/22/Stanley_Baldwin_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="378" data-file-height="515" /></a><figcaption>Baldwin, unknown date</figcaption></figure>
<p>In May 1923 Bonar Law was diagnosed with terminal cancer and retired immediately; he died five months later. With many of the party's senior leading figures standing aloof and outside of the government, there were only two candidates to succeed him: <a href="/wiki/George_Nathaniel_Curzon,_1st_Marquess_Curzon_of_Kedleston" class="mw-redirect" title="George Nathaniel Curzon, 1st Marquess Curzon of Kedleston">Lord Curzon</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Secretary" title="Foreign Secretary">foreign secretary</a>, and Baldwin. The choice formally fell to King <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">George V</a> acting on the advice of senior ministers and officials.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>It is not entirely clear what factors proved most crucial, but some Conservative politicians felt that Curzon was unsuitable for the role of prime minister because he was a member of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Lords" title="House of Lords">House of Lords</a>. Curzon was strong and experienced in international affairs, but his lack of experience in domestic affairs, his personal character quirks and his huge inherited wealth and many directorships at a time when the Conservative Party was seeking to shed its patrician image were all deemed impediments. Much weight at the time was given to the intervention of <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Balfour" title="Arthur Balfour">Arthur Balfour</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The King turned to Baldwin to become prime minister. Initially Baldwin was also <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> whilst he sought to recruit the former Liberal Chancellor <a href="/wiki/Reginald_McKenna" title="Reginald McKenna">Reginald McKenna</a> to join the government. When this failed he appointed <a href="/wiki/Neville_Chamberlain" title="Neville Chamberlain">Neville Chamberlain</a> to that position.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="1923_general_election">1923 general election</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: 1923 general election">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Conservatives now had a clear majority in the <a href="/wiki/British_House_of_Commons" class="mw-redirect" title="British House of Commons">House of Commons</a> and could govern for five years before holding a general election, but Baldwin felt bound by Bonar Law's pledge at the previous election that there would be no introduction of tariffs without a further election. Thus Baldwin turned towards a degree of <a href="/wiki/Protectionism" title="Protectionism">protectionism</a> which would remain a key party message during his lifetime.<sup id="cite_ref-mc_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mc-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> With the country facing growing unemployment in the wake of free trade imports driving down prices and profits, Baldwin decided to call an early general election in <a href="/wiki/1923_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1923 United Kingdom general election">December 1923</a> to seek a mandate to introduce protectionist tariffs which, he hoped, would drive down unemployment and spur an economic recovery.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> He expected to unite his party but he divided it, for protectionism proved a divisive issue.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>  The election was inconclusive: the Conservatives had 258 MPs, Labour 191 and the reunited Liberals 159. Whilst the Conservatives retained a plurality in the House of Commons, they had been clearly defeated on the central issue: tariffs.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Baldwin remained prime minister until the opening of the new Parliament in January 1924, when his administration was defeated in a vote on its legislative programme set out in the <a href="/wiki/State_Opening_of_Parliament" title="State Opening of Parliament">King's Speech</a>. He offered his resignation to <a href="/wiki/George_V" title="George V">George V</a> immediately.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Leader_of_the_Opposition_.281924.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Leader_of_the_Opposition_(1924)">Leader of the Opposition (1924)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Leader of the Opposition (1924)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Baldwin successfully held on to the party leadership amid some colleagues' calls for his resignation.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> For the next ten months, an unstable minority Labour government under Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ramsay_MacDonald" title="Ramsay MacDonald">Ramsay MacDonald</a> held office. On 13 March 1924, the Labour government was defeated for the first time in the Commons, although the Conservatives decided to vote with Labour later that day against the Liberals.<sup id="cite_ref-Cowling1_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cowling1-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During a debate on the naval estimates the Conservatives opposed Labour but supported them on 18 March in a vote on cutting expenditure on the Singapore military base.<sup id="cite_ref-Cowling1_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cowling1-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Baldwin also cooperated with MacDonald over Irish policy to stop it becoming a party-political issue.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Labour government was negotiating with the Soviet government over intended commercial treaties – 'the Russian Treaties' – to provide <a href="/wiki/Most_favoured_nation" title="Most favoured nation">most favoured nation</a> privileges and diplomatic status for the UK trade delegation; and a treaty that would settle the claims of pre-revolutionary British bondholders and holders of confiscated property, after which the British government would guarantee a loan to the Soviet Union.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Baldwin decided to vote against the government over the Russian Treaties, which brought the government down on 8 October.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="1924_re-election">1924 re-election</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: 1924 re-election">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/1924_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1924 United Kingdom general election">general election</a> held in October 1924 brought a landslide majority of 223 for the Conservative party, primarily at the expense of an unpopular <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Party_(UK)" title="Liberal Party (UK)">Liberal Party</a>. Baldwin campaigned on the "impracticability" of socialism, the <a href="/wiki/Campbell_Case" title="Campbell Case">Campbell Case</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Zinoviev_letter" title="Zinoviev letter">Zinoviev letter</a> (which Baldwin thought was genuine, and the Conservatives leaked to the <i>Daily Mail</i> at a most damaging time to the Labour campaign; the letter is now widely believed to have been a forgery<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>) and the Russian Treaties.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> In a speech during the campaign Baldwin said:
</p>
<blockquote><p>It makes my blood boil to read of the way which Mr. Zinoviev is speaking of the Prime Minister today. Though one time there went up a cry, "Hands off Russia", I think it's time somebody said to Russia, "Hands off England".<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span id="Prime_minister:_second_term_.281924.E2.80.931929.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_minister:_second_term_(1924–1929)">Prime minister: second term (1924–1929)<span class="anchor" id="Second_premiership"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Prime minister: second term (1924–1929)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cabinet">Cabinet</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Stanley_Baldwin&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Cabinet">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+link+.ambox{margin-top:-1px}html body.mediawiki .mw-parser-output .ambox.mbox-small-left{margin:4px 1em 4px
